{"parentUuid":"bc25597e-4f52-4f6d-ae80-c9ac75331d01","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"ae42573080b47b5cd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016xVv3Nw6pbCcQtWmFBCgg2","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Symlink-safe staging for ``LocalDir`` manifest uploads.\n2\t\n3\tThe sandbox SDK's ``LocalDir`` walker refuses to copy symlinks at all — it\n4\traises ``LocalDirReadError(reason=\"symlink_not_supported\")`` on the first one\n5\tas a path-escape / TOCTOU safeguard. Real source trees (especially JS/TS\n6\tmonorepos with workspace or shared-config links) routinely commit symlinks, so\n7\thanding such a tree straight to ``LocalDir`` aborts the upload before the agent\n8\teven starts.\n9\t\n10\t:func:`stage_symlink_safe_dir` returns a path that is always safe to hand to\n11\t``LocalDir``:\n12\t\n13\t* a tree with no symlinks is used as-is (no copy);\n14\t* otherwise the tree is copied into a temp directory with symlinks resolved:\n15\t\n16\t  - a link whose target stays inside the tree is *dereferenced* (its target\n17\t    content is materialized in place), so the agent still sees the file;\n18\t  - a link that escapes the tree, dangles, or forms a cycle is *dropped* and\n19\t    never followed. Refusing to follow out-of-tree links preserves the walker's\n20\t    path-escape safety and keeps host/out-of-tree content from leaking into the\n21\t    (hostile) sandbox.\n22\t\n23\tRegular files are hard-linked when possible (falling back to a copy across\n24\tdevices), so the staged tree adds negligible disk for the non-symlink bulk.\n25\t\"\"\"\n26\t\n27\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n28\t\n29\timport logging\n30\timport os\n31\timport shutil\n32\timport tempfile\n33\tfrom pathlib import Path\n34\t\n35\t\n36\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n37\t\n38\t_STAGING_PREFIX = \"strix-localdir-\"\n39\t\n40\t\n41\tdef _is_within(target: Path, root: Path) -> bool:\n42\t    \"\"\"Return whether ``target`` is ``root`` itself or nested under it.\"\"\"\n43\t    if target == root:\n44\t        return True\n45\t    try:\n46\t        target.relative_to(root)\n47\t    except ValueError:\n48\t        return False\n49\t    return True\n50\t\n51\t\n52\tdef tree_has_symlink(root: Path) -> bool:\n53\t    \"\"\"Return whether ``root`` contains any symlink (file or directory).\"\"\"\n54\t    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root, followlinks=False):\n55\t        base = Path(dirpath)\n56\t        for name in (*dirnames, *filenames):\n57\t            if (base / name).is_symlink():\n58\t                return True\n59\t    return False\n60\t\n61\t\n62\tdef _link_or_copy(src: Path, dst: Path) -> None:\n63\t    \"\"\"Hard-link ``src`` to ``dst``, falling back to a content copy.\"\"\"\n64\t    try:\n65\t        os.link(src, dst)\n66\t    except OSError:\n67\t        shutil.copy2(src, dst, follow_symlinks=True)\n68\t\n69\t\n70\tdef _stage_dir(src: Path, dst: Path, root: Path, seen: frozenset[Path]) -> None:\n71\t    dst.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n72\t    for entry in os.scandir(src):\n73\t        entry_path = Path(entry.path)\n74\t        dest_path = dst / entry.name\n75\t\n76\t        if entry.is_symlink():\n77\t            target = Path(os.path.realpath(entry_path))\n78\t            if not _is_within(target, root):\n79\t                logger.warning(\"staging: dropping out-of-tree symlink %s -> %s\", entry_path, target)\n80\t                continue\n81\t            if not target.exists():\n82\t                logger.warning(\"staging: dropping dangling symlink %s\", entry_path)\n83\t                continue\n84\t            if target in seen:\n85\t                logger.warning(\"staging: dropping cyclic symlink %s -> %s\", entry_path, target)\n86\t                continue\n87\t            if target.is_dir():\n88\t                _stage_dir(target, dest_path, root, seen | {target})\n89\t            else:\n90\t                _link_or_copy(target, dest_path)\n91\t        elif entry.is_dir(follow_symlinks=False):\n92\t            _stage_dir(entry_path, dest_path, root, seen)\n93\t        elif entry.is_file(follow_symlinks=False):\n94\t            _link_or_copy(entry_path, dest_path)\n95\t        else:\n96\t            # Sockets, FIFOs, devices — not part of a source tree; skip.\n97\t            logger.debug(\"staging: skipping non-regular entry %s\", entry_path)\n98\t\n99\t\n100\tdef stage_symlink_safe_dir(src_root: Path) -> tuple[Path, Path | None]:\n101\t    \"\"\"Return ``(upload_path, staged_temp)`` for uploading ``src_root``.\n102\t\n103\t    ``upload_path`` is safe to hand to ``LocalDir``. When the tree contains no\n104\t    symlinks it is ``src_root`` itself and ``staged_temp`` is ``None``.\n105\t    Otherwise a symlink-safe copy is materialized in a temp directory and both\n106\t    returned values point at it; the caller owns removing ``staged_temp`` once\n107\t    the upload completes.\n108\t    \"\"\"\n109\t    root = src_root.resolve()\n110\t    if not tree_has_symlink(root):\n111\t        return root, None\n112\t\n113\t    staged = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=_STAGING_PREFIX))\n114\t    try:\n115\t        _stage_dir(root, staged, root, frozenset({root}))\n116\t    except OSError:\n117\t        shutil.rmtree(staged, ignore_errors=True)\n118\t        raise\n119\t    logger.info(\"staging: materialized symlink-safe copy of %s at %s\", root, staged)\n120\t    return staged, staged\n121\t"}]},"uuid":"c1a313db-8ac6-4992-85c3-3100b901232d","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:21:27.315Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"bc25597e-4f52-4f6d-ae80-c9ac75331d01","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

source_path = _override or _DEFAULT_PATH\n37\t        init_kwargs: dict[str, Any] = _read_json_overrides(source_path)\n38\t        _cached = Settings(**init_kwargs)\n39\t        logger.debug(\n40\t cache.\"\"\"\n50\t    global _override, _cached  # noqa: PLW0603\n51\t    _override = path\n52\t    _cached = None\n53\t    logger.info(\"config override applied: %s\", path)\n54\t\n55\t\n56\tdef persist_current() -> None:\n57\t    \"\"\"Write currently-set env vars to the active config file (0o600).\"\"\"\n58\t    s = load_settings()\n59\t    target = _override or _DEFAULT_PATH\n60\t    target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n61\t\n62\t    env_block: dict[str, str] = {}\n63\t    for sub_name in s.model_fields:\n64\t        sub_model = getattr(s, sub_name)\n65\t        if not isinstance(sub_model, BaseModel):\n66\t            continue\n67\t        for finfo in type(sub_model).model_fields.values():\n68\t            for alias in _aliases_for(finfo):\n69\t                value = os.environ.get(alias.upper())\n70\t                if value:\n71\t                    env_block[alias.upper()] = value\n72\t                    break\n73\t\n74\t    target.write_text(json.dumps({\"env\": env_block}, indent=2), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n75\t    with contextlib.suppress(OSError):\n76\t        target.chmod(0o600)\n77\t\n78\t\n79\tdef _aliases_for(finfo: FieldInfo) -> list[str]:\n80\t    \"\"\"Collect every env-var name that should populate ``finfo``.\"\"\"\n81\t    aliases: list[str] = []\n82\t    if finfo.alias:\n83\t        aliases.append(finfo.alias)\n84\t    va = finfo.validation_alias\n85\t    if isinstance(va, AliasChoices):\n86\t        aliases.extend(c for c in va.choices if isinstance(c, str))\n87\t    elif isinstance(va, str):\n88\t        aliases.append(va)\n89\t    return aliases\n90\t\n91\t\n92\tdef _read_json_overrides(path: Path) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:\n93\t    \"\"\"Read ``{\"env\": {...}}`` from ``path`` and remap to nested kwargs.\n94\t\n95\t    Only includes keys whose env var is NOT already set, so env always\n96\t    wins over the persisted file.\n97\t    \"\"\"\n98\t    if not path.exists():\n99\t        return {}\n100\t    try:\n101\t        data = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n102\t    except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError):\n103\t        return {}\n104\t    env_block = data.get(\"env\", {}) if isinstance(data, dict) else {}\n105\t    if not isinstance(env_block, dict):\n106\t        return {}\n107\t\n108\t    env_block_upper = {str(k).upper(): v for k, v in env_block.items()}\n109\t    env_present = {k.upper() for k in os.environ}\n110\t\n111\t    nested: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}\n112\t    for sub_name, sub_finfo in Settings.model_fields.items():\n113\t        sub_cls = sub_finfo.annotation\n114\t        if not (isinstance(sub_cls, type) and issubclass(sub_cls, BaseModel)):\n115\t            continue\n116\t any(alias in env_present for alias in aliases):\n120\t                continue  # env wins under some alias; skip the JSON file for this field\n121\t            for alias in aliases:\n122\t errors}\n46\t\n47\t    try:\n48\t        from strix.report.state import get_global_report_state\n49\t\n50\t        report_state = get_global_report_state()\n51\t        if report_state is None:\n52\t technical_analysis=technical_analysis.strip(),\n63\t            recommendations=recommendations.strip(),\n64\t        )\n65\t        vuln_count = len(report_state.vulnerability_reports)\n66\t    except (ImportError, AttributeError) as e:\n67\t        logger.exception(\"finish_scan persistence failed\")\n68\t        return {\"success\": False, \"error\": f\"Failed to complete scan: {e!s}\"}\n69\t    else:\n70\t        logger.info(\n71\t            \"finish_scan: completed scan with %d vulnerability report(s)\",\n72\t            vuln_count,\n73\t        )\n74\t        return {\n75\t            \"success\": True,\n76\t            \"scan_completed\": True,\n77\t            \"message\": \"Scan completed successfully\",\n78\t            \"vulnerabilities_found\": vuln_count,\n79\t        }\n80\t\n81\t\n82\t@function_tool(timeout=60)\n83\tasync def finish_scan(\n84\t    ctx: RunContextWrapper,\n85\t    executive_summary: str,\n86\t    methodology: str,\n87\t    technical_analysis: str,\n88\t    recommendations: str,\n89\t) -> str:\n90\t    \"\"\"Finalize the scan — persist the customer-facing report.\n91\t\n92\t    **Root-agent only.** Subagents must call ``agent_finish`` from the\n93\t    multi-agent graph tools instead. Calling this finalizes everything:\n94\t\n95\t    1. Verifies you are the root agent.\n96\t    2. Writes the four narrative sections to the scan record.\n97\t    3. Marks the scan completed and stops execution.\n98\t\n99\t    **This is a terminal action, not a status probe.** Whatever you pass\n100\t    is persisted VERBATIM as the final, customer-facing report and then\n101\t    execution stops. There is no draft mode and no second chance: never\n102\t    submit placeholder, provisional, or \"checking if done\" text in any\n103\t    field, and never call ``finish_scan`` to poll whether subagents are\n104\t    done (use ``view_agent_graph`` / ``wait_for_message`` for that).\n105\t    Call it exactly ONCE, only when every field holds genuine, finished\n106\t    assessment prose.\n107\t\n108\t    **Pre-flight checklist (mandatory — do not skip):**\n109\t\n110\t    1. **Call ``view_agent_graph`` first.** Inspect every entry in the\n111\t       summary. If ANY agent is in ``running`` / ``waiting`` state,\n112\t       you MUST NOT call ``finish_scan`` yet —\n113\t       wrap them up first via ``send_message_to_agent`` (ask them to\n114\t       finish), ``wait_for_message`` (block until their report\n115\t       arrives), or ``stop_agent`` (graceful cancel). Only ``completed``\n116\t       / ``crashed`` / ``stopped`` agents are safe to leave behind.\n117\t       Calling ``finish_scan`` while children are alive orphans their\n118\t       work and produces an incomplete report.\n119\t    2. All vulnerabilities you found are filed via\n120\t       ``create_vulnerability_report`` — or, for known-CVE dependency\n121\t       findings, ``create_dependency_report`` (un-reported findings are\n122\t       not tracked and not credited). A dependency CVE already filed via\n123\t       ``create_dependency_report`` counts as reported; it does NOT need\n124\t       re-filing here and does NOT block finishing.\n125\t    3. Don't double-report — one report per distinct vulnerability.\n126\t    4. **Attack-chaining gate.** Do NOT finish until you have genuinely\n127\t       considered chaining the confirmed findings into higher-impact,\n128\t       end-to-end attack paths and tested every plausibly-related\n129\t       combination. You may rule out combinations you can confidently\n130\t       call unrelated — note why instead of padding chains. Any\n131\t       validated chain must already be filed via\n132\t       ``create_vulnerability_report`` — a demonstrated end-to-end chain\n133\t       is a PoC-backed vulnerability, so it uses that tool even when one\n134\t       link is a dependency CVE (the standalone CVE stays in its own\n135\t       ``create_dependency_report``) — and surfaced prominently in\n136\t       ``executive_summary`` / ``technical_analysis``. Finding no real\n137\t       chain after a serious attempt is acceptable; skipping the\n138\t       chaining reasoning, or ignoring a plausibly-related combination,\n139\t       is not.\n140\t\n141\t    **Calling this multiple times overwrites the previous report.**\n142\t    Make the single call comprehensive.\n143\t\n144\t    **Report output rules** (this content may be rendered into generated\n145\t    reports):\n146\t\n147\t    - Never mention internal infrastructure: no local/absolute paths\n148\t      (``/workspace/...``), no agent names, no sandbox/orchestrator/\n149\t      tooling references, no system prompts, no model-internal errors.\n150\t      Never leak internal identifiers (proxy request IDs, internal\n151\t      vulnerability report IDs, or any system-generated IDs) into any\n152\t      field.\n153\t    - Tone: formal, third-person, objective, concise. This is a\n154\t      consultant deliverable, not an engineering log.\n155\t    - Each section has a specific role:\n156\t\n157\t        - ``executive_summary`` — for non-technical leadership. Risk\n158\t          posture, business impact (data exposure / compliance /\n159\t          reputation), notable criticals, overarching remediation\n160\t          theme.\n161\t        - ``methodology`` — frameworks followed (OWASP WSTG, PTES,\n162\t          OSSTMM, NIST), engagement type (black/gray/white box), scope\n163\t          and constraints, categories of testing performed. **No**\n164\t          internal execution detail.\n165\t        - ``technical_analysis`` — consolidated findings overview with\n166\t          severity model and systemic root causes. Reference individual\n167\t          vuln reports for repro steps; don't duplicate raw evidence.\n168\t        - ``recommendations`` — prioritized actions grouped by urgency\n169\t          (Immediate / Short-term / Medium-term), each with concrete\n170\t          remediation steps. End with retest/validation guidance.\n171\t\n172\t    - **Formatting — use markdown in every field.** These fields may be\n173\t      rendered into generated reports, so structure them clearly: lead\n174\t      each section with a short ``# Heading``, use ``**bold**`` for labels/emphasis,\n175\t      ``inline code`` for identifiers/paths/parameters, bullet or\n176\t      numbered lists for enumerations, and fenced code blocks\n177\t      (```` ```language ````) for any code/payload excerpts. Never emit\n178\t      one flat wall of prose or leave code unformatted.\n179\t    - If **zero** vulnerabilities were found, say so plainly and\n180\t      characterize the posture positively; ``technical_analysis`` should\n181\t      summarize the areas tested and confirm no issues, and\n182\t      ``recommendations`` should focus on general hardening.\n183\t\n184\t    Example (abbreviated — mirror this structure, not the wording)::\n185\t\n186\t        executive_summary:\n187\t            # Executive Summary\n188\t\n189\t            An external assessment of the **Acme Customer Portal**\n190\t            identified multiple weaknesses that could lead to\n191\t            unauthorized access to customer data.\n192\t\n193\t            **Overall risk posture:** Elevated.\n194\t\n195\t            **Key findings**\n196\t            - Confirmed SSRF in a URL-preview feature reaching internal\n197\t              network ranges.\n198\t            - Broken tenant isolation enabling cross-tenant data access.\n199\t\n200\t            **Business impact**\n201\t            - Potential exposure of customer records across tenants.\n202\t\n203\t        methodology:\n204\t            # Methodology\n205\t\n206\t            Conducted per the **OWASP WSTG**.\n207\t\n208\t            **Engagement type:** Gray-box external test.\n209\t            **Scope:** `https://app.acme.example`, `.../api/v1/`.\n210\t\n211\t            **Activities:** recon, authn/session review, authorization\n212\t            and tenant-isolation testing, input/SSRF testing.\n213\t\n214\t        technical_analysis:\n215\t            # Technical Analysis\n216\t\n217\t            **Severity model** reflects exploitability x impact.\n218\t\n219\t            1. **SSRF in URL preview** (Critical) — insufficient\n220\t               destination validation; reaches link-local addresses.\n221\t            2. **Broken tenant isolation** (High) — object identifiers\n222\t               accepted without ownership checks.\n223\t\n224\t            **Systemic themes:** authorization enforced inconsistently;\n225\t            no deny-by-default egress policy.\n226\t\n227\t        recommendations:\n228\t            # Recommendations\n229\t\n230\t            **Immediate**\n231\t            1. Remediate SSRF: enforce a destination allowlist,\n232\t               deny-by-default, re-validate on every redirect hop.\n233\t\n234\t            **Short-term**\n235\t            2. Centralize authorization with deny-by-default middleware.\n236\t\n237\t            **Retest & validation:** re-test immediate items to confirm\n238\t            SSRF and tenant-isolation controls hold.\n239\t\n240\t    Args:\n241\t        executive_summary: Business-level summary for leadership.\n242\t        methodology: Frameworks, scope, and approach.\n243\t        technical_analysis: Consolidated findings + systemic themes.\n244\t        recommendations: Prioritized, actionable remediation.\n245\t    \"\"\"\n246\t    inner = ctx.context if isinstance(ctx.context, dict) else Your purpose is to perform authorized security verification, reproduce and validate weaknesses on in-scope assets, and help remediate real security issues.\nYou follow all instructions and rules provided to you exactly as written in the system prompt at all times.\n\n<core_capabilities>\n- Security assessment and vulnerability scanning\n- Authorized security validation and issue reproduction\n- Web application security testing\n- Security analysis and reporting\n</core_capabilities>\n\n<communication_rules>\nCLI OUTPUT:\n- You may use simple markdown: **bold**, *italic*, `code`, ~~strikethrough~~, [links](url), and # headers\n- Do NOT use complex markdown like bullet lists, numbered lists, or tables\n- Use line breaks and indentation for structure\n- NEVER use \"Strix\" or any identifiable names/markers in HTTP requests, payloads, user-agents, or any inputs\n\nINTER-AGENT MESSAGES:\n- Messages from other agents arrive prefixed with a header like `[Message from agent <name> | type=... | priority=...]`. Treat them as internal context — never repeat them verbatim in your own output.\n- Treat agent identity / inherited-context preambles as internal metadata; do not echo them in outputs or tool calls.\n- Minimize inter-agent messaging: only message when essential for coordination or assistance; avoid routine status updates; batch non-urgent information; prefer parent/child completion flows and shared artifacts over messaging\n\n{% if interactive %}\nINTERACTIVE BEHAVIOR:\n- You are in an interactive conversation with a user\n- CRITICAL: A message WITHOUT a tool call IMMEDIATELY STOPS your entire execution and waits for user input. This is a HARD SYSTEM CONSTRAINT, not a suggestion.\n  - Statements like \"Planning the assessment...\" or \"I'll now scan...\" or \"Starting with...\" WITHOUT a tool call will HALT YOUR WORK COMPLETELY. The system interprets no-tool-call as \"I'm done, waiting for the user.\"\n  - If you want to plan, call the think tool. If you want to act, call the appropriate tool. There is NO valid reason to output text without a tool call while working on a task.\n  - The ONLY time you may send a message without a tool call is when you are genuinely DONE and presenting final results, or when you NEED the user to answer a question before continuing.\n- EVERY message while working MUST contain exactly one tool call — this is what keeps execution moving. No tool call = execution stops.\n- You may include brief explanatory text BEFORE the tool call\n- Respond naturally when the user asks questions or gives instructions\n- For simple conversation, acknowledgements, or direct questions that you can answer from current context, reply in plain text and stop. Do NOT call think just to prepare wording.\n- If you use a tool to answer a user question (for example list_todos, view_agent_graph, or a file read), then after the tool result arrives, provide the answer in plain text and stop unless the user explicitly asked you to continue working.\n- Never loop through think or other tools just to prepare, polish, confirm, or announce a final answer. Once you know the answer, say it.\n- NEVER send empty messages — if you have nothing to do or say, call the wait_for_message tool\n- If you catch yourself about to describe multiple steps without a tool call, STOP and call the think tool instead\n{% else %}\nAUTONOMOUS BEHAVIOR:\n- Work autonomously by default\n- You should NOT ask for user input or confirmation - you should always proceed with your task autonomously.\n- Minimize user messaging: avoid redundancy and repetition; consolidate updates into a single concise message\n- NEVER send an empty or blank message. If you have no content to output or need to wait (for user input, subagent results, or any other reason), you MUST call the wait_for_message tool (or another appropriate tool) instead of emitting an empty response.\n- If there is nothing to execute and no user query to answer any more: do NOT send filler/repetitive text — either call wait_for_message or finish your work (subagents: agent_finish; root: finish_scan)\n- While the agent loop is running, almost every output MUST be a tool call. Do NOT send plain text messages; act via tools. If idle, use wait_for_message; when done, use agent_finish (subagents) or finish_scan (root)\n- A text-only turn — even one — IMMEDIATELY ends the scan/run with no report written. {"parentUuid":"c98a33d7-5764-4b36-adb9-b8e89b215286","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ae42573080b47b5cd","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdCS9fEVeUmryKXVUFwdG","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I now have a complete picture of the execution and sandbox architecture. Here is my in-depth analysis.\n\n---\n\n# How Strix Works: Execution + Sandbox Deep-Dive\n\nStrix is a thin, opinionated orchestration layer built **on top of the OpenAI Agents SDK** (`openai-agents==0.14.6`, pinned — see `strix/runtime/docker_client.py:19`). The SDK provides the agent primitive, the LLM turn/tool-call loop, streaming, session memory, run hooks, and — critically — a first-class **sandbox** abstraction (`SandboxAgent`, `Shell`/`Filesystem` capabilities, `DockerSandboxClient`). Strix's own code is essentially: (a) a coordinator for an addressable multi-agent graph, (b) a Docker/Kali subclass of the SDK sandbox client, (c) a suite of `function_tool`s, and (d) prompt/config/reporting glue.\n\nThe whole thing runs in **one asyncio event loop**; every agent is an `asyncio.Task`; concurrency between parent and children is cooperative multitasking, not threads.\n\n---\n\n## 1. The Agent Loop\n\n### 1a. How an agent is constructed — `strix/agents/factory.py`\n\nThe single constructor is `build_strix_agent()` (`factory.py:402-472`). It returns an SDK `SandboxAgent`:\n\n```python\nreturn SandboxAgent(\n    name=name,\n    instructions=instructions,                 # rendered Jinja system prompt\n    tools=tools,                               # base tools + extras + lifecycle tool\n    tool_use_behavior=_finish_tool_use_behavior,   # <-- key: controls \"when to stop\"\n    model=None,                                # model comes from RunConfig, not the agent\n    capabilities=[\n        Filesystem(configure_tools=...),\n        Shell(configure_tools=_make_shell_configurator(...)),\n    ],\n)\n```\n\nKey construction facts:\n\n- **Tools** (`factory.py:329-357`, `_BASE_TOOLS`): a fixed tuple of `function_tool`-decorated coroutines — `think`, `load_skill`, todo/notes CRUD, `web_search`, vulnerability/dependency reporting, Caido proxy tools (`list_requests`, `repeat_request`, …), and the **agents-graph tools** (`view_agent_graph`, `send_message_to_agent`, `wait_for_message`, `create_agent`, `stop_agent`). Then the terminal lifecycle tool is appended per role: `finish_scan` for root, `agent_finish` for children (`factory.py:438-441`).\n- **`Shell` + `Filesystem` capabilities** are what give the agent `exec_command`, `write_stdin`, file read/write/patch tools. These tools are **injected by the SDK** and executed *inside the sandbox container* (see §3). Strix wraps them via `configure_tools` hooks to (i) default `shell=\"bash\"` on `exec_command` (`factory.py:208-232`), (ii) decode escape sequences in `write_stdin` (`factory.py:235-252`), and (iii) turn SDK \"custom tools\" into plain JSON function tools when the backend can't accept Responses-style custom tools (`_configure_chat_completions_filesystem_tools`, `factory.py:162-168`).\n- **`model=None`**: the agent is model-agnostic. The model is supplied per-run via `RunConfig` (§5), so the same agent object works with any LiteLLM provider.\n- **Child factory** (`make_child_factory`, `factory.py:475-502`): a closure capturing run-level config (`scan_mode`, `is_whitebox`, etc.) so `create_agent` can mint children without knowing runner internals.\n\n### 1b. How the loop is wired to the SDK — the \"stop\" decision\n\nThe pivotal piece is `tool_use_behavior=_finish_tool_use_behavior` (`factory.py:307-326`). Normally an SDK agent loop ends when the model emits a message with **no tool call** (a \"final output\"). Strix overrides this: after each batch of tool results, `_finish_tool_use_behavior` inspects them and returns `ToolsToFinalOutputResult(is_final_output=True, ...)` **only** when a lifecycle tool succeeded:\n\n```python\nfor tool_result in tool_results:\n    if _lifecycle_tool_completed(tool_result.tool.name, tool_result.output):  # finish_scan / agent_finish\n        return ToolsToFinalOutputResult(is_final_output=True, final_output=tool_result.output)\n    if interactive and _wait_tool_parked(tool_result.tool.name, tool_result.output):  # wait_for_message\n        return ToolsToFinalOutputResult(is_final_output=True, final_output=tool_result.output)\nreturn ToolsToFinalOutputResult(is_final_output=False, final_output=None)   # keep looping\n```\n\nSo the agent decides to **finish** by *calling a tool* (`finish_scan`/`agent_finish`), not by emitting prose. `_lifecycle_tool_completed` (`factory.py:276-290`) parses the tool's JSON output and checks `success && (scan_completed|agent_completed)`. This is why the prompt is so emphatic that \"a text-only turn immediately ends the run with no report\" — in non-interactive mode a bare text final output is treated as an error and forcibly recovered (see below).\n\n### 1c. How an LLM turn + tool dispatch actually runs — `strix/core/execution.py`\n\nEvery turn cycle goes through `_run_cycle()` (`execution.py:338-447`). It calls the SDK's **streaming** runner:\n\n```python\nstream = Runner.run_streamed(\n    agent, input=input_data, run_config=run_config,\n    context=context, max_turns=max_turns, session=session, hooks=hooks,\n)\nawait coordinator.attach_stream(agent_id, stream)\nasync for event in stream.stream_events():\n    if event_sink is not None:\n        event_sink(agent_id, event)     # -> TUI rendering\nif stream.run_loop_exception is not None:\n    raise stream.run_loop_exception\n```\n\n`Runner.run_streamed` is the SDK's driver: it manages the model call → tool-call dispatch → tool execution → append-to-session → repeat, honoring `tool_use_behavior`. Strix just **consumes the event stream** (forwarding to the TUI via `event_sink`) and lets the SDK do the internal turn management. `max_turns` (default **500**, `inputs.py:24`) caps model calls per cycle.\n\n`_run_cycle` wraps this with substantial resilience logic:\n- **Image-budget enforcement** before each cycle (`enforce_image_budget`, `execution.py:356-362`) — keeps only the last N screenshot tool-outputs in the SDK session (default 3, `settings.py:60`), eliding older ones to bound context.\n- **Input-rejection recovery** (`execution.py:411-430`): on a 400/404/422 from the provider, it strips all images from the session and retries up to 3×.\n- **LiteLLM \"after shutdown\" race** and **sandbox teardown errors** are swallowed (`execution.py:387-401`).\n- **Budget stop** (`BudgetExceededError`) propagates a scan-wide halt (`execution.py:404-410`).\n- On success, `_settle_run_result` (`execution.py:450-464`) parks the agent as `waiting` (interactive) or leaves it `running`.\n\n### 1d. How the agent decides to continue vs finish — two loop shapes\n\n`run_agent_loop()` (`execution.py:45-122`) has two modes:\n\n- **Non-interactive** (`_run_noninteractive_until_lifecycle`, `execution.py:270-335`): loops `_run_cycle` repeatedly. After each cycle it checks status; if the agent is still `running` (i.e. produced a text-only final output instead of calling a lifecycle tool), it counts an \"invalid final output,\" injects a corrective user message (\"call exactly one tool… `finish_scan`/`agent_finish`\", `execution.py:482-504`), and retries — up to `max_turns` attempts before raising `MaxTurnsExceeded` and marking `crashed`. This guarantees the run only ends through a lifecycle tool.\n- **Interactive** (`execution.py:99-122`): runs one cycle, then **parks** and awaits `coordinator.wait_for_message(agent_id)`. When a message arrives it drains pending and runs another cycle with `input_data=[]` (the SDK replays the session, which now includes the injected message). This is the chat/TUI model — the agent is an event-driven actor.\n\n### 1e. Concurrency model (asyncio)\n\n- One event loop for the whole scan. In the TUI it runs on a dedicated thread with its own loop (`app.py:1447-1468`); in non-interactive CLI it's `asyncio.run(run_cli(args))` (`main.py:921`).\n- **Each agent = one `asyncio.Task`.** The root runs inline in `run_strix_scan`; every child is spawned as a detached task via `asyncio.create_task(_child_loop(), name=f\"agent-{name}-{child_id}\")` (`execution.py:585`).\n- **Shared state is guarded by a single `asyncio.Lock`** in `AgentCoordinator._lock` (`agents.py:45`). All status/graph/message mutations take it.\n- **Per-session write lock**: out-of-band writes to an SDK session (message delivery) are serialized by `session_write_lock` (`sessions.py:58-67`, a `WeakKeyDictionary[Session, Lock]`), so a peer appending a message never races the SDK's own turn writes.\n- Parallelism is **cooperative**: children make progress whenever the parent awaits (e.g. `wait_for_message`). True parallel LLM calls happen because each task independently awaits its own network I/O.\n\n### 1f. Hooks — `strix/core/hooks.py`\n\n`ReportUsageHooks(RunHooks)` (`hooks.py:27-71`) is passed to every `Runner.run_streamed` call. It implements one SDK lifecycle callback, `on_llm_end`, invoked after **every model response**. It:\n1. Records SDK-native token usage into the global `ReportState` per `agent_id`/model (`hooks.py:56-64`).\n2. Enforces the **budget**: if cumulative LLM cost ≥ `max_budget_usd`, it raises `BudgetExceededError` (`hooks.py:66-71`), which unwinds the stream, triggers `coordinator.trigger_budget_stop()` (wakes every parked agent so they exit — `agents.py:60-65`), and cleanly stops the scan (`runner.py:374-380`). This is the only run-hook Strix uses; cost accounting also flows through a **LiteLLM success callback** registered in config (`models.py:191-202`).\n\n---\n\n## 2. Multi-Agent Orchestration (\"agents graph\")\n\n### 2a. The coordinator — `strix/core/agents.py`\n\n`AgentCoordinator` (`agents.py:35-321`) is the **single owner of all graph state**. It holds parallel dicts keyed by 8-char `agent_id`:\n- `statuses` (`running|waiting|completed|stopped|crashed|failed`), `parent_of`, `names`, `metadata` (task + skills), `pending_counts` (inbox depth), and `runtimes`.\n- Each `AgentRuntime` (`agents.py:26-32`) bundles the agent's SDK `Session`, its `asyncio.Task`, its active stream, an `interrupt_on_message` flag, and an `asyncio.Event wake`.\n\nIt also **persists a JSON snapshot** after every state change (`_maybe_snapshot`, `agents.py:301-321`, atomic temp-file replace to `.state/agents.json`), which is what enables `--resume`.\n\n### 2b. Spawning a child — `create_agent` tool → `spawn_child_agent`\n\nThe model calls `create_agent(name, task, inherit_context, skills)` (`agents_graph/tools.py:364-472`). That tool:\n1. Validates skills, grabs `parent_history = list(ctx.turn_input)` if `inherit_context` (`tools.py:442`).\n2. Pulls the runner-injected `spawn_child_agent` callable out of context (`tools.py:415`) and invokes it.\n\nThe runner wires `spawn_child_agent` as a closure in `run_strix_scan` (`runner.py:269-281`) that delegates to `execution.spawn_child_agent` (`execution.py:125-186`):\n- Mints `child_id = uuid4().hex[:8]`, builds the child agent via the factory, `coordinator.register(child_id, name, parent_id, task, skills)`.\n- Builds the child's **initial input** (`child_initial_input`, `inputs.py:151-186`): a single user message containing the scrubbed parent history (\"background only — do not continue the parent's work\"), an identity line (\"You are agent {name} ({child_id}); your parent is {parent_id}… call agent_finish when done\"), and the task.\n- Opens a fresh SDK `SQLiteSession` for the child (shared `agents.db`, keyed by `child_id`), attaches it, and starts a detached `_child_loop` task (`execution.py:533-587`). The parent's `create_agent` returns immediately — **the child runs in parallel**.\n\n### 2c. Parent/child relationships & message passing\n\n- **Topology** is `parent_of` (root has `parent=None`). Subtree traversal via `_subtree_order_locked` (`agents.py:272-279`).\n- **Messages are delivered by appending to the target's SDK session** — `coordinator.send()` (`agents.py:125-156`). A peer message is rendered as a `{\"role\":\"user\"}` item prefixed with `[Message from {name} ({id}) | type=… | priority=…]` (`_message_to_session_item`, `agents.py:253-270`). Delivery increments `pending_counts`, sets the target's `wake` event, and — if the target is mid-turn and `interrupt_on_message` (interactive) — calls `stream.cancel(mode=\"immediate\")` so the agent picks the message up on its next cycle.\n- **Inter-agent tools** (`agents_graph/tools.py`): `send_message_to_agent` (targeted inbox delivery), `view_agent_graph` (renders the indented tree with statuses, marks caller `← you`), `wait_for_message` (park until inbox non-empty or timeout — the autonomous path uses `asyncio.wait_for(coordinator.wait_for_message, timeout_seconds)`, `tools.py:317-332`).\n- **Completion reporting is automatic**: when a child calls `agent_finish` (`tools.py:475-589`), it renders a structured completion report and `coordinator.send()`s it to the parent's inbox with `type=completion, priority=high`, then sets its own status `completed`. Parents typically `wait_for_message` to collect these.\n\n### 2d. Coordination & termination\n\n- **Root termination**: `finish_scan` (`finish/tool.py:82-286`) refuses if any child is still `running`/`waiting` (`active_agents_except`, `finish/tool.py:250-268`), persists the four-section executive report, and marks the scan complete.\n- **Stopping a subtree**: `stop_agent(target, cascade)` (`tools.py:592-687`) → `cancel_descendants_graceful` walks the subtree leaves-first calling `request_stop` on each, which sets status `stopped` and cancels the stream with `mode=\"after_turn\"` (`agents.py:185-195`) — the current turn finishes and saves to the session before the loop honors the cancel.\n- **Hard cancel** on failure/budget: `cancel_descendants` (`agents.py:197-207`) cancels the actual `asyncio.Task`s and gathers them.\n- **Crash propagation**: `_notify_parent_on_crash` (`execution.py:507-530`) messages the parent so it stops waiting on a dead child.\n- **Resume**: on restart, `respawn_subagents` (`execution.py:189-268`) reads the restored snapshot and re-launches every non-terminal child task from its persisted SDK session, replaying full history with `initial_input=[]`.\n\n---\n\n## 3. The Docker Kali Sandbox\n\n### 3a. Provisioning — `session_manager.create_or_reuse` (`runtime/session_manager.py:70-147`)\n\nPer `scan_id`, cached in `_SESSION_CACHE`. It:\n1. Splits `local_sources` into **copied manifest entries** vs **bind mounts** (`build_session_entries`, `session_manager.py:32-67`). Sources flagged `mount` become read-only bind mounts at `/workspace/<subdir>`; everything else becomes an SDK `LocalDir` entry (file-by-file copy), first passed through symlink-safe staging.\n2. Builds an SDK `Manifest` whose `Environment` injects the **in-container proxy env** — `http_proxy/https_proxy/ALL_PROXY = http://127.0.0.1:48080` (Caido), `NO_PROXY=localhost,127.0.0.1` (`session_manager.py:94-107`). Any process launched via `session.exec` inherits these, so all HTTP(S) traffic is auto-routed through Caido.\n3. Selects the backend (`get_backend(\"docker\")` → `_docker_backend`, `backends.py:20-55`), which constructs `StrixDockerSandboxClient`, calls `client.create(...)` then **`session.start()`** — the latter is what actually materializes the manifest (copies LocalDir entries, applies volume/FUSE mounts) into the running container.\n4. Resolves the exposed Caido port to a host URL and **bootstraps Caido** (§3d).\n\nThe result bundle `{client, session, caido_client}` is cached and returned to the runner, which packs `client` + `session` into `SandboxRunConfig` (`runner.py:224`).\n\n### 3b. The Docker subclass — `runtime/docker_client.py`\n\n`StrixDockerSandboxClient` (`docker_client.py:151-278`) subclasses the SDK's `DockerSandboxClient` and **re-implements `_create_container` verbatim from the SDK source** (because the SDK exposes no hook to extend `create_kwargs`), with these deltas:\n1. **Preserve the image ENTRYPOINT**: instead of the SDK's `entrypoint=[\"tail\"]` override, it sets `command=[\"tail\",\"-f\",\"/dev/null\"]` (`docker_client.py:183`). This lets `docker-entrypoint.sh` run (starting Caido, trusting the CA) and then `exec \"$@\"` becomes the keep-alive.\n2. **Append `NET_ADMIN` + `NET_RAW`** capabilities (`docker_client.py:211-217`) — needed for `nmap -sS` and other raw-socket tools.\n3. **Add `host.docker.internal → host-gateway`** to `extra_hosts` (`docker_client.py:219-220`) so the agent can reach host-served target apps.\n\nIt also applies **opt-in resource caps** from env (`_apply_resource_limits`: `STRIX_SANDBOX_MEM_LIMIT/SHM_SIZE/CPUS/PIDS_LIMIT`, `docker_client.py:69-91`) and a **default-on log cap** (`_apply_log_limits`: 50m×3 json-file rotation, so a runaway stdout can't fill host disk, `docker_client.py:93-110`). Bind mounts from `--mount` are appended as read-only Docker `Mount`s (`docker_client.py:227-239`). `delete()` does a best-effort `container.kill()` before super-delete (`docker_client.py:264-278`).\n\nOptional network isolation: `STRIX_DOCKER_SANDBOX_NETWORK` puts the container on a named network and switches port resolution to container-IP (`StrixDockerSandboxSession._resolve_exposed_port`, `docker_client.py:113-148`).\n\n### 3c. The Kali image — `containers/Dockerfile`\n\nBase `kalilinux/kali-rolling:latest`. Runs as non-root user **`pentester`** with passwordless sudo (`Dockerfile:10-13`). Notable installed tooling:\n- **Recon/scan**: `nmap` (+ setcap raw/admin, `Dockerfile:40`), `ncat`, `sqlmap`, `nuclei` (+ templates), `subfinder`, `naabu`, `ffuf`, ProjectDiscovery `httpx`/`katana`/`cvemap(vulnx)`/`interactsh-client`, `gospider`, `dirsearch`, `arjun`, `wafw00f`.\n- **DAST/SAST/secrets**: `zaproxy`, `wapiti`, `semgrep`, `bandit`, `trufflehog`, `gitleaks`, `trivy`, `retire`, `eslint`, `jshint`, `ast-grep`, tree-sitter parsers (Java/JS/TS/Python/Go/Bash/JSON/YAML).\n- **Browser automation**: `chromium` + `agent-browser@0.26.0` (CDP-driven headless browser CLI) with screenshot dir `/workspace/.agent-browser-screenshots` (`Dockerfile:90-96`).\n- **Runtime**: python3+venv at `/app/.venv` with `caido-sdk-client`, `httpx`, `pyjwt`, `cryptography`, etc.; `uv`; Go; Node/npm. `jwt_tool`, JS-Snooper, jsniper cloned to `/home/pentester/tools`.\n- **Caido CLI** v0.56.0 (`Dockerfile:173-186`) — the intercepting proxy sidecar.\n- A **self-signed \"Testing Root CA\"** is generated and added to the system trust store + `REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE`/`SSL_CERT_FILE` (`Dockerfile:43-62,188-189`) so Caido can MITM TLS transparently.\n- `strix/tools/proxy/caido_api.py` is copied to `/opt/strix-python` and put on `PYTHONPATH` (`Dockerfile:205-206`) so in-sandbox Python scripts can drive Caido.\n\n### 3d. Entrypoint & Caido bootstrap — `containers/docker-entrypoint.sh` + `runtime/caido_bootstrap.py`\n\n`docker-entrypoint.sh` starts `caido-cli --listen 0.0.0.0:48080 --allow-guests --import-ca-cert …` in the background (`entrypoint.sh:25-34`), waits up to 30s for its GraphQL endpoint, writes the proxy env to `/etc/profile.d/proxy.sh`, `/etc/environment`, `/etc/wgetrc`, and `~/.bashrc/.zshrc` (`entrypoint.sh:66-100`), adds the CA to the Chromium NSS trust store (`entrypoint.sh:104-108`), then `cd /workspace && exec \"$@\"` (the keep-alive tail).\n\nHost-side, `bootstrap_caido` (`caido_bootstrap.py:83-109`) fetches a guest token by **`session.exec`-ing curl inside the container** against the GraphQL `loginAsGuest` mutation (retry loop doubles as readiness probe, `caido_bootstrap.py:34-80`), then builds a host-side `caido_sdk_client.Client` pointed at the runtime's exposed-port URL and creates/selects a temporary \"sandbox\" project. This host client backs the proxy tools (`list_requests`, `repeat_request`, `list_sitemap`, …).\n\n### 3e. How the agent executes commands *inside* the container\n\nThis is pure SDK plumbing that Strix rides on: `RunConfig.sandbox = SandboxRunConfig(client=…, session=…)` (`runner.py:224`) plus the agent's `Shell`/`Filesystem` capabilities. When the model calls `exec_command`/file tools, the SDK routes them to `session.exec(...)` in the container. Because the manifest injected proxy env vars, all outbound HTTP from those commands transits Caido automatically. Strix never re-implements exec — it just configures/wraps these SDK tools (`_configure_shell_tools`, `factory.py:255-266`) and mounts the workspace.\n\n### 3f. Isolation / security model\n\n- **Container = the trust boundary.** The sandbox is treated as **hostile** (comments in `local_dir_staging.py:20-21`): symlinks that escape the source tree are dropped so host content can't leak in; local copies are size-capped (`STRIX_MAX_LOCAL_COPY_MB`, default 1024, `settings.py:58`) forcing large repos to read-only bind mounts.\n- Runs as unprivileged `pentester` (though with sudo). Extra caps limited to `NET_ADMIN/NET_RAW` (plus `SYS_ADMIN` only if a FUSE/overlay manifest demands it).\n- **Scope enforcement is prompt-level**, not network-level: the system prompt injects a \"SYSTEM-VERIFIED SCOPE\" block of authorized targets (`build_scope_context`, `inputs.py:97-122`; rendered at `system_prompt.jinja` top) and instructs the agent never to touch off-scope assets. `_merge_root_prompt_context`/`_compose_root_instructions_override` (`runner.py:55-98`) guarantee user-supplied root instructions **cannot override** the scope block. There is no egress firewall — isolation is the container + resource/log caps + prompt scope.\n- Per-scan container, killed and reaped on teardown (`session_manager.cleanup`, `session_manager.py:150-186`).\n\n### 3g. Multiple sessions\n\nSessions are keyed by `scan_id` in `_SESSION_CACHE` (`session_manager.py:26`). One container per scan; **all agents in a scan share that one container** and its Caido. Within the scan, per-*agent* isolation is only at the SDK-session (LLM memory) level — every agent gets its own `SQLiteSession` row in the shared `agents.db`, but they execute shell/tools in the same shared Kali container/workspace.\n\n---\n\n## 4. End-to-End Control-Flow Trace\n\n`strix/interface/main.py:main()` (`main.py:843`):\n1. `parse_arguments()` — targets (`--target/--target-list/--mount`), `--instruction`, `--non-interactive`, `--scan-mode {quick,standard,deep}`, `--scope-mode`, `--max-budget-usd`, `--resume`, `--config` (`main.py:443-561`).\n2. `check_docker_installed()` → `pull_docker_image()` → `validate_environment()` → `warm_up_llm()` (`main.py:854-858`). Warm-up does a real 1-token round-trip through `StrixProvider().get_model()` and warns on unknown/non-frontier models (`main.py:269-378`).\n3. For fresh runs: clone repos, `collect_local_sources` (marks `mount` sources), resolve PR diff-scope, persist a run record (`main.py:862-906`).\n4. Dispatch: `asyncio.run(run_cli(args))` (non-interactive) or `asyncio.run(run_tui(args))` (`main.py:920-923`).\n\n`interface/cli.py:run_cli` (or the TUI's `_start_scan_thread`):\n5. Assembles `scan_config` (targets, instructions, scan_mode, local_sources, diff_scope, resume_instruction — `cli.py:85-97`).\n6. Creates `ReportState`, registers it globally (`set_global_report_state`) so tools/hooks can record findings & cost (`cli.py:99-135`).\n7. Calls `run_strix_scan(scan_config, scan_id, image=_resolve_sandbox_image(), local_sources, interactive, max_budget_usd, [event_sink])` (`cli.py:180-187`; TUI passes `coordinator`, `interactive=True`, `event_sink=self._capture_sdk_event`, `app.py:1458-1468`).\n\n`core/runner.py:run_strix_scan` (`runner.py:101-412`) — the spine:\n8. Sets up `run_dir` + `.state/` (`agents.json`, `agents.db`), detects resume, sets up logging (`runner.py:128-137`).\n9. `load_settings()` + `configure_sdk_model_defaults(settings)` (LiteLLM wiring, §5) → resolves the model (`runner.py:149-157`).\n10. Creates/restores the `AgentCoordinator`; hydrates todos/notes from disk (`runner.py:159-194`).\n11. **`await session_manager.create_or_reuse(...)`** → the Kali container comes up, Caido bootstraps (§3) (`runner.py:198-204`).\n12. Builds `ModelSettings` (`make_model_settings`: `parallel_tool_calls=False`, retry policy, reasoning effort, timeout — `inputs.py:125-148`) and the SDK **`RunConfig`** binding model + `StrixProvider` + `SandboxRunConfig(client, session)` (`runner.py:214-226`).\n13. Instantiates `ReportUsageHooks` (budget + usage) (`runner.py:227`).\n14. Builds the **root agent** with the scope-injected prompt (`build_strix_agent(is_root=True, …)`, `runner.py:240-250`); registers root in coordinator; builds `make_child_factory` and the `spawn_child_agent` closure; assembles the **context dict** (coordinator, sandbox_session, caido_client, agent_id, spawn_child_agent, max_context_images) that every tool receives via `RunContextWrapper` (`runner.py:283-292`).\n15. Opens the root SDK session; if resuming, `respawn_subagents` (`runner.py:294-311`).\n16. **`await run_agent_loop(root_agent, initial_input=root_task, …)`** (`runner.py:339-352`) → drives `_run_cycle` → `Runner.run_streamed` → LLM turns → tool calls (`exec_command` etc. into the container; `create_agent` spawns children as parallel tasks) → until the root calls `finish_scan`.\n17. On finish, verifies `scan_completed` (logs an error if the model ended text-only, `runner.py:353-373`); on budget/rate-limit/exception it cancels descendants and sets root status; **`finally`** closes SDK sessions and (if `cleanup_on_exit`) tears down the container (`runner.py:401-411`).\n18. Back in `main`: cleanup `ReportState`, emit telemetry, `display_completion_message`, and in non-interactive mode exit 2 if any vulns were found (`main.py:931-948`).\n\nKey responsibilities: **runner** = wiring/lifecycle; **execution** = per-agent loop + resilience; **coordinator** = graph state + messaging; **factory** = agent/tool assembly; **session_manager/docker_client** = sandbox; **hooks** = usage/budget.\n\n---\n\n## 5. State + Config — `strix/config/`\n\n### settings.py — pydantic-settings models\n`Settings` composes four sub-models (`settings.py:75-81`):\n- **`LlmSettings`** (`settings.py:20-43`): `model` (`STRIX_LLM`), `api_key` (`LLM_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`), `api_base` (`LLM_API_BASE`/`OPENAI_BASE_URL`/`OLLAMA_API_BASE`/…), `reasoning_effort` (`STRIX_REASONING_EFFORT`, default `high`, one of none/minimal/low/medium/high/xhigh), `force_required_tool_choice`, `timeout` (300s).\n- **`RuntimeSettings`** (`settings.py:46-60`): `image` (`STRIX_IMAGE`, default `ghcr.io/usestrix/strix-sandbox:1.0.0`), `backend` (`docker`), `max_local_copy_mb` (1024), `max_context_images` (3).\n- **`TelemetrySettings`**, **`IntegrationSettings`** (Perplexity key).\n\n### loader.py — precedence & persistence\n`load_settings()` (`loader.py:29-45`, memoized) resolves **env > JSON file (`~/.strix/cli-config.json`) > defaults**. `_read_json_overrides` only pulls a JSON value if no env alias is set (`loader.py:92-127`). `persist_current()` writes currently-set env vars back to the config file at 0600 (`loader.py:56-76`). `apply_config_override(path)` swaps the source and busts the cache.\n\n### models.py — LiteLLM / provider wiring (the important part)\n- **`StrixProvider(MultiProvider)`** (`models.py:39-60`): routes bare/`openai/` names to OpenAI, and **any other `provider/model` prefix through LiteLLM with the prefix preserved** (so users type `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro`, `anthropic/claude-opus-4-8`, not `litellm/…`). `ollama/x` → `ollama_chat/x`. This provider is handed to `RunConfig.model_provider`, so the whole `openai-agents[litellm]` integration is driven from here.\n- **`configure_sdk_model_defaults`** (`models.py:116-131`): disables tracing; configures LiteLLM (`drop_params`, `modify_params`, `turn_off_message_logging`, registers a **cost callback** `litellm_cost_callback`, `models.py:153-165,191-202`); sets OpenAI key/base; **mirrors the single API key into the correct provider env var** via `litellm.validate_environment` (`models.py:134-151`); and picks the SDK API surface: **`responses`** API for OpenAI-native, **`chat_completions`** whenever `api_base` is set. `uses_chat_completions_tool_schema` (`models.py:212-219`) decides whether tools must be plain JSON function tools (any non-openai prefix, any custom `api_base`, or a non-reasoning model) — which feeds the `chat_completions_tools` flag through the factory (§1a).\n- **Retry** (`DEFAULT_MODEL_RETRY`, `models.py:63-77`): 5 retries, exp backoff 2→90s, on 429/5xx/network/provider-suggested/statusless errors.\n- **`RECOMMENDED_MODEL_NAMES` / `FRONTIER_MODEL_FAMILIES`** (`models.py:79-113`) drive the warm-up quality warning; reasoning support is probed from `litellm.model_cost` (`models.py:222-233`).\n\n### Runtime/persistent state\n- **LLM memory** = SDK `SQLiteSession` per agent, all in `.state/agents.db` (`sessions.py:24-26`).\n- **Graph topology/status** = `.state/agents.json` (atomic snapshots, `agents.py:301-321`) — the resume backbone.\n- **Findings/cost/report** = global `ReportState` (todos/notes hydrated from disk, vuln reports, executive report written by `finish_scan`).\n\n---\n\n## Mental model in one paragraph\n\nStrix = **OpenAI Agents SDK + a graph coordinator + a Kali Docker sandbox**. Each agent is a model-agnostic `SandboxAgent` whose loop is the SDK's `Runner.run_streamed`; Strix only overrides *when the loop stops* (`tool_use_behavior` → only on a successful `finish_scan`/`agent_finish`) and wraps the shell/filesystem tools. All agents share one asyncio loop and one Kali container; each is an `asyncio.Task` with its own SQLite LLM session, coordinated through a single `AgentCoordinator` (status dicts + inbox = messages appended to a peer's SDK session, guarded by one lock). `create_agent` spawns children as parallel tasks that inherit scrubbed parent history and auto-report completion to the parent's inbox. Shell/file tool calls execute inside the container via `session.exec`, with all HTTP forced through an in-container Caido proxy (transparent TLS MITM via a baked-in CA). The runner owns lifecycle: bring up the sandbox, build `RunConfig` (model via `StrixProvider`/LiteLLM + `SandboxRunConfig`), run the root loop under budget/usage hooks, persist an atomic graph snapshot for `--resume`, and tear the container down. Config is env-over-JSON pydantic settings; the LLM layer is entirely LiteLLM-routed so any provider works by prefix.\n\n### Ideas most relevant to a SIGIL + CRUCIBLE fusion\n- The **coordinator-as-actor-graph** pattern (addressable agents, inbox = append-to-session, atomic JSON snapshot for crash-resume) is a clean, provider-agnostic multi-agent core worth lifting wholesale (`core/agents.py`, `core/execution.py`).\n- The **lifecycle-tool-as-terminator** invariant (`_finish_tool_use_behavior` + non-interactive forced-recovery) is a robust way to guarantee structured completion from otherwise free-form agents.\n- The **SDK-subclass sandbox** approach (`StrixDockerSandboxClient`) shows how to bolt hardening (caps, log/resource limits, bind mounts, network isolation, entrypoint sidecar) onto a stock sandbox client without forking the SDK.\n- The **transparent-proxy-via-injected-env + baked CA** design gives full HTTP capture for free to any subprocess — directly reusable for an offensive engine's evidence/oracle
